# ⛔ NON-NEGOTIABLE OPERATING RULES — READ FIRST, EVERY COMMAND, NO EXCEPTIONS

These override everything else. Violating scope is worse than doing nothing. When in doubt: STOP and report.

1. SCOPE LOCK. Work ONLY on the exact task in the current instruction. Do not touch, edit, refactor, rename, reformat, "improve," clean up, or fix ANY file, feature, module, or behaviour outside that exact task — not even if it looks broken, related, or trivial, and not even if you are "already in the file."

2. NO AUTO-FIX / REPORT-ONLY OUTSIDE SCOPE. If you find a bug, regression, or issue anywhere outside your exact task, STOP and REPORT it to the conductor with exact file:line + root cause. Do NOT change it. Nothing outside the assigned task is changed without Johan's strict, specific, explicit instruction.

3. SPEC-EXACT, NO IMPROVISING. Build strictly to the instruction and the named .ai/specs/ spec. Add NOTHING that was not explicitly asked for — no extra features, fields, pages, UI, or behaviour. If the instruction and the spec conflict, or anything is ambiguous, STOP and ask the conductor. Never guess. Never interpret. Never assume.

4. STAY IN YOUR LANE. Work only in your assigned module. Never wander into another part of CoreX for any reason.

5. TWO QA LANES — THE LANE OWNER GATES STAGING, JOHAN GATES LIVE. Johan's work lands on QA1 (branch `QA1`); Andre's work lands on QA2 (branch `QA2`). Work STAYS on its lane until the lane owner has tested it there and given an explicit go. Flow: QA1 -> Johan tests -> Johan's go -> Staging; QA2 -> Andre tests -> Andre's go -> Staging. A lane never promotes the other lane's work. Nothing goes from Staging to live, and no live work of any kind (code OR data) happens, without Johan's specific explicit order for that exact action. (Amended 2026-09-16 on Andre's instruction — QA2 is a real lane, not a sandbox.)

6. NO SILENT EXTRAS. No speculative changes, no "while I was here," no drive-by refactors, no dependency bumps, no formatting sweeps, no touching unrelated files.

7. REPORT EXACTLY. When done, report exactly what changed (files + why) and how you proved it, and confirm nothing outside the task was touched.

8. FULL CRUD, LIST-SCREEN COMPLETENESS, AND OWN/BRANCH/AGENCY SCOPING ARE THE FLOOR — DESIGNED IN, NOT REQUESTED. Johan's words: "we always need proper crud? search / sort / own / branch / agency levels. that should be the design standard. not me asking for it once we get to that stage." Every entity ships with Create, Read, Update, Archive (soft delete only — never hard delete) and Restore from the first build, not as a later ask. Every list screen ships with search (named fields), sort (every sensible column + a stated default), filter (status + date range minimum), pagination, and a real empty state. Every list, detail view, export, download, and API endpoint enforces OWN / BRANCH / AGENCY visibility scoping at the query layer (BelongsToAgency / AgencyScope, never a hidden UI link) — direct-URL access by ID is blocked, not just unlinked. The spec for any new feature states search fields, sort/default, filters, and per-screen scoping BEFORE code is written; a spec missing these is not ready to build. Full detail: BUILD_STANDARD.md §1a.

9. MULTI-AGENCY ALWAYS, NEVER SINGLE. Johan, 2026-09-19, verbatim: "the important part is that whatever we do is multi agency. not single. never." A second real agency — Cape Town, mainly rentals, signed to start October 2026 — is about to run on the same rentals code that has so far only ever been built and tested against agency 1 (HFC). Every place something is HFC's by assumption instead of genuinely per-agency is a place that new agency will find, in front of a paying customer.
   • No feature, screen, template, document, email, default, or setting may assume one agency.
   • No hardcoded agency IDs, no agency-1 defaults, no HFC-specific wording, branding, addresses, or signatures anywhere in shipped code.
   • Every default must be neutral and sensible for an agency that is not HFC.
   • Anything an agency would reasonably want different about its own operation is a configurable setting, not a code constant — this extends the existing no-hardcoding rule beyond thresholds and time windows to cover wording, documents, and branding too.
   • The test, every time you build or review anything: what does this look like for the SECOND agency? If the answer is "wrong" or "HFC's," it is not done.

This applies to the conductor too.

> Last updated: 2026-10-17 by **AT-456 Part D — Post Scheduler (QA2; Johan): on the Ad Manager results step each ad has Schedule this post, and there is a Schedule N ads button for the whole batch. The user ticks Facebook and/or Instagram (a platform that is not connected is greyed out with a plain 'not connected — connect it under My Portal, Tools, Social Media' message), picks a date and time (at least 5 minutes ahead, at most 60 days) or, for several ads, 'one a day at HH:MM' starting on a chosen day, and CoreX publishes each post itself at that time through the existing Meta connection — not Facebook's own scheduler. A fourth Ad Manager tab, Scheduled Posts, lists them (picture, property, platform, agent, time in South African time, status, text; search, sort soonest-first by default, filters for status/platform/dates/agent, 15 per page): Edit text or time while waiting, Cancel (kept on the list, never deleted), Reschedule a cancelled one. An agent sees only their own posts, a branch manager their branch, an admin the agency; another person's post by id is a 404. If a post cannot go out (connection expired or removed, listing sold, picture gone, Facebook refuses) it is marked failed with a plain reason, the agent gets a bell notification, and it is never retried automatically (it could double-post). New Role Manager permission 'Schedule social posts' (agents, branch managers, office admins by default; granted to existing roles by migration). The publisher is the command marketing:publish-due (every minute where a scheduler runs; QA2 has none and no queue worker, by Johan's choice, so run php artisan marketing:publish-due by hand there); pictures are kept until published + 30 days (marketing:purge-scheduled-images, daily). New event MarketingPostScheduleFailed. Not in the setup wizard (no new agency setting). Spec at456-ai-tools-scheduler-and-spend-safety.md §D.7. > Last updated: 2026-10-13 by **AT-452 NO OVERRUN ALLOWED (Johan: "if its set to R750 then thats a hard lock"; spec `rentals-faults-work-orders.md` §22.6): the authorised amount is a hard lock. The tolerance-% pass is REMOVED from the cost gate - any final cost, variation (extra work, higher quote, crew price change) or invoice even R0.01 above it goes to the owner as a request for the extra and the job cannot complete/close until it is decided (owner link, or the agent recording the owner's instruction); exactly the amount passes. Call-out fee vs the owner's call-out allowance, the repair limit and the emergency limit are compared in whole cents (no epsilon). The stored tolerance work term is no longer read: removed from Settings > Rental work orders (and the Setup Wizard step), the per-property 'Work terms agreed with the owner' panel + route, the lease hub 'Extra work passed without asking' line and the 'extra passed within tolerance' owner email; the data is kept (old auto-approved extras read as 'passed earlier (old agreement)'). > Prior: 2026-10-13 by **AT-452 'nothing is automatic' stage 2 - Approve the cost (QA1 only; Johan; spec `rentals-faults-work-orders.md` §22.6 + §22.14): a selected quote or a priced crew card APPROVES NOTHING any more. The work order and the own-crew job card carry one box, 'Approve the cost' (two equal buttons: 'Authorise R___ within the repair limit' / 'Record the owner's decision'); the amount is the selected quote, else our own crew's work-order selling price (the job card cost is never read or shown to the owner), else an amount the agent types for a small known job. Inside the owner's repair limit (`RentalOwnerFaultSettings`, null = must ask) the agent authorises (record `agent_repair_limit`, owner emailed at once); 'This is an emergency' + a required reason uses the emergency limit (`agent_emergency_limit`); above the limit - or with no limit - the owner decides first on their link or via 'Record the owner's decision' (Approved up to R___ / Declined, written down, emailed). The old Emergency approval form is gone (old agreements stay readable/voidable). A final cost above what was authorised sends the DIFFERENCE to the owner (request for the extra, close waits); the repair limit never approves extra money, only the owner's own variation tolerance. Public methods other lanes build on: `RentalAuthorisationService::costMeasure/costEvaluation/authoriseCost/recordOwnerCostDecision/hasCostAuthority` (spec §22.13). The agent's queue gains 'Authorise the cost' rows (work order and own-crew card). The old rule's wording is gone everywhere (settings 'No-approval spend limit' is now 'Suggested repair limit' - only a suggestion; the limits shown on screens are the owner's own, with who said so). No migration. Tests `AuthorityCostTest`.**
> Last updated: 2026-10-13 by **AT-452 'nothing is automatic' stage 3 - Stop this / Query this (QA1 only; Johan): every owner notice about something an agent did without the owner deciding (send to inspect, recorded owner decision; stage 2's 'authorised under limit' gets it free through the same mail) carries two equal buttons, in the email (signed links to pages that ASK; only the owner's own press records, scanners cannot) and on the owner portal at 390px. 'Stop this' (reason required + confirm) works until the work has started; it PAUSES the job - red PAUSED BY OWNER on the fault, work order and job card, every send/approve/start refused with the reason (one hold check in the gate, the contractor send, the crew link, both Decision 1 buttons, send-to-owner, agent approval), both lease agents alerted in-app AND emailed, the contractor/crew already sent the job emailed 'On hold - do not attend until you hear from us', the tenant's progress reads On hold, the authorisation records gain 'Stopped by owner <name> on <date time>: <reason>'. Resume needs a fresh authority: the owner's 'Go ahead after all' or the agent's Resume (= Record the owner's decision popup). 'Query this' stops nothing: both agents alerted in-app + email, block 'Owner's question' with an Answer popup on the fault page, answer emailed and shown on the owner's link/portal. One service `RentalOwnerHoldService`; stage 2 MUST call `holdReason()` before its own approve actions. Spec `rentals-faults-work-orders.md` §22.7 + §22.12a; tests `OwnerStopQueryTest`. Schema snapshot not re-dumped (migration 2026_10_28_200000).**

See the Recent decisions log.
> Prior: 2026-09-29 by **Syndication Approval Gate — layer 3: a chosen person must approve a listing before it is syndicated** (spec `.ai/specs/syndication-approval-gate.md`; branch `syndication-approval-gate`, NOT on any lane yet, awaiting Johan's test). CoreX had two automatic gates between a listing and the portals (on-market, then compliance); Johan asked for a third that is a human being. An agency switches it on in **Settings → Properties → Syndication Approval** and ticks who approves; from then on a compliance-GREEN listing still cannot reach Property24, Private Property or an agency website until one of them says yes. Agent flow: once compliance is done a **Send for approval** button appears beside Compliance Status (and in the syndication panel, with an optional note); pressing it emails every chosen approver ("Approval needed: 12 Beach Road, Uvongo" — price, agent, note, and links to the property and to the queue) and the listing carries an amber **Awaiting approval** tag. Approver flow: the **Properties list IS the queue** (Johan's D7 — no separate screen) via a new "Awaiting approval" count tile + `?filter=approval_pending`, both visible only to approvers; Approve / Reject (reason required) / Withdraw approval sit on the property. **Johan's four rulings, all built:** D1 one approval covers every portal; D2 approval holds FOREVER — editing price/photos/description afterwards does NOT re-trigger it, asserted explicitly so nobody adds re-approval later; D3 it holds back all syndication (P24, PP, every agency website, 8 enable paths); D8 "only new stock" — switching it on stamps everything already live on a portal as approved, each with an audit row reading "already published when syndication approval was switched on", so the approver never faces the back catalogue. Deliberately narrow within D8: a listing that has never gone out still needs one nod (approval never expires, so grandfathering it would leave a permanent hole), and a deliberate human revoke is never resurrected by an off→on cycle. The property's own `status` column is NEVER written — it is the exact word CoreX sends the portals, so approval is its own tag beside it; a test asserts it. Revoke stops a listing going anywhere NEW and does not pull it off a portal it already reached. Bulk console syndication (`p24:bulk-syndicate`, `pp:bulk-syndicate`) skips unapproved stock and reports the count, so a bulk run cannot launder a listing past the approver. 2 migrations (`properties.syndication_approved_at` + `property_syndication_approvals`), 1 permission (`properties.syndication.manage_approvers` — who may CONFIGURE the gate; who may APPROVE is the agency's own roster, deliberately not a second list that could drift), 4 domain events in the catalogue + explicit `Event::listen` registrations (discovery is off). **OFF by default — an agency that never switches it on sees no badge, no button, no tile and runs no extra query.** Tests: `SyndicationApprovalGateTest` 19/19, `SyndicationApprovalQueueTest` 13/13 green (the second verified again from a BRAND-NEW database, proving the regenerated schema snapshot loads); all 8 touched Blades compile-checked; 5 routes resolve. Full suite NOT run (Rule 13). **Setup Wizard — DONE, Johan ruled 2026-09-29 that it belongs there** (spec §9.1, revision 4). It needed a genuinely new wizard control type: `user_multiselect`, a live per-agency people picker, because the wizard could previously only render STATIC option maps and "who approves" is this agency's own user list. Plus a `perf_json` value source for a JSON-valued PerformanceSetting. **It also forced a real fix worth knowing about for every future wizard step:** `AgencySetupWizardController::save()` IGNORES a saver's return value — it reacts only to a thrown `ValidationException`. The "choose at least one approver before you turn this on" refusal was a `redirect()->with('error')`, which the wizard would have swallowed whole: step marked complete, setting silently never written — the exact `agency-onboarding-setup.md` §6.1 failure. It now THROWS, so both the wizard and the settings page show a field-level error. A saver that refuses by RETURNING anything is a saver the wizard cannot refuse with. The empty hidden `key[]` companion is load-bearing for the same class in reverse (un-ticking the last person must post an empty array, not nothing). `SyndicationApprovalWizardTest` 7/7; pre-existing `AgencySetupWizardSaverGuardTest` 9/9 still green; driven in a real Chrome (refusal on screen with the gate left OFF in the DB, then a successful save, then re-open showing what was saved, siblings intact, 0 console errors). **Also on the record:** spec revision 3 §6.2 records why the authority check is a service gate rather than the Laravel Gate the first draft proposed: the codebase has zero `Gate::define`/`@can`, so a framework Gate would have been a second authorisation idiom for one feature.
> Prior: 2026-09-28 by **Setup Wizard bug fixed as a class, not an instance — `AgencySetupWizardController::currentValues()` audited end to end.** Follow-up to the Job 3 report: the `'rental_inspections'` arm silently ignoring `public_link_expiry_days`/`auto_pair_photos_enabled`/`auto_send_report_enabled` was the SAME shape the 2026-09-20 fix already named once (a source with a single hardcoded resolver call, or none at all, instead of dispatching per `$key`) — Johan asked for every other arm to be checked for the same class, not just those three. Found a SECOND live instance: `'leases'` was hardcoded to always return `expiryNoticeWindowDaysFor()`, so its second control, `default_deposit_months`, silently displayed the expiry-window value instead of its own. `'rental_work_orders'` had the identical shape with no live bug yet (only one control exists there today, but two more are already slated to land under it per its own spec) — hardened to the same explicit-per-key-match shape anyway. Every arm in `currentValues()` is now either genuinely generic (resolves any key via a real column/method-name lookup — `perf`/`deal_sync`/`proforma`/`mailbox`/`rental_application`, verified gap-free) or an explicit `match ($key)` naming every control the config actually declares under that source (`leases`/`rental_work_orders`/`rental_inspections`/`rental_inventories`). Also fixed, found running the existing regression test: its own 'leases'-step payload had drifted behind several savers added to that step over time (the three toggle checkboxes above, plus `approval_mode`/`require_checklist_complete` from an unrelated earlier AT-430 change) — each has()-guards or validates its own field and rejects the WHOLE step save when absent, so the test was already broken before this pass touched it, just never re-run since those savers landed. New structural test walks every control the config declares and asserts each explicit-per-key source's own match arm names it, not just "does an arm exist" — the exact gap every instance of this bug was. 3/3 tests green. Spec: `.ai/specs/agency-onboarding-setup.md` §6.2 (the general rule, for the next time a control gets added under an existing source), `.ai/specs/rental-inventory.md` §17 updated from "reported" to "fixed." Landed at `2b33a1d5f`, deployed via `scripts/qa-deploy.sh`.)

Verified directly against real property 15726 on live QA1 (its existing draft inventory's `distributionRecipients()` correctly resolved the real seller contact by email, the public page and PDF both rendered). **Reported, not fixed (non-negotiable #2):** `AgencySetupWizardController::currentValues()`'s pre-existing `'rental_inspections'` match arm silently ignores `public_link_expiry_days`/`auto_pair_photos_enabled`/`auto_send_report_enabled` (all three added in `2fd77ff40`), always rendering their hardcoded wizard default regardless of the agency's real saved value — same bug class a comment two lines above already documents fixing once, just not re-applied to these three; exact file:line in spec `.ai/specs/rental-inventory.md` §17. Landed at `46039aa8e`, deployed via `scripts/qa-deploy.sh`.)
> Prior: 2026-09-28 by **"Create spaces from listing" — one-click room seeding on the blank Inventory tab, reusing the Inspections seeder.** Johan verified 15726's Inventory tab opens (previous fix), but it still said "no spaces set up yet" on a listing with real bedrooms/bathrooms/garages/flatlets — "why ask the agent to retype something we know." Investigated first, found `RentalInspectionFormSeeder::seedFromAdvertising()` already does exactly this for Inspections' own blank state, reading the property's advertising Spaces (`spaces_json`) — confirmed 15726's own `spaces_json` already lists Bedroom×6, Bathroom×5, Parking×6, Kitchen×3, **Flatlet×2** ("Flatlet 1"/"Flatlet 2"), Lounge×3, and more — real structured data no beds/baths/garages count column would ever expose. Wired the SAME endpoint (`RentalInventoryCaptureController` now passes the existing `seed-from-advertising` route to the view) rather than building a second seeder: `capture.blade.php`'s blank state gained a "Create spaces from listing" button (confirm-gated, error surfaced through the existing `spaceError` slot, never silent) that merges every returned room into the Alpine state exactly like the manual "Add space" control already does. > Prior: 2026-09-20 by **PDF Splitter review screen — the inapplicable filing button is now absent, not dead.** Johan reported "Link to Contact" greyed out on `/tools/pdf-splitter/review` with contacts visibly ticked. Nothing was broken in the filing path: the screen has two mutually exclusive doors — "Link" (needs a property; files each page to the property AND its ticked contacts) and "Link to Contact" (needs an anchor contact, whose search box only renders when NO property is picked). With a property picked, "Link to Contact" could never enable, yet sat on screen wearing the word "contact" right after the agent finished ticking contacts, with a tooltip telling them to "pick a contact above first" when there is no picker above. Each button is now wrapped in `<template x-if>` (`!anchorContact` / `!property`) so only the applicable one renders; with neither picked BOTH still show, disabled, so both doors stay discoverable. `x-if` not `x-show` on purpose — `tour-engine.blade.php` filters steps with `document.querySelector(s.element)`, so a merely `display:none` `data-tour="spr-link"` anchor would leave the tour highlighting an invisible button. View-only change, one file, no controller/route/DB touched; `:disabled` bindings deliberately left exactly as they were, so the hidden button stays disabled and can never become the form's implicit Enter-key submitter. **Built directly on `Prod` (the live box, `/corex` @ 62.238.31.82) on Johan's explicit "make the fix on prod" order** — it has NOT been through QA1 → Staging, so forward-port it or the next promotion silently reverts it.

> Prior: 2026-09-15 by **Branch archive + agent reassignment (AT-420) built on its own branch — deals carry on as loaded, moves are dated, archived branches keep their names and can be restored** (previous: a manual `prospecting:rehydrate-thumbnails` run put 5,226 `DownloadListingThumbnail` jobs on `default`, whose worker is numprocs=1. Network-bound at ~0.65 jobs/sec that is ~2h15m of downloads, and a database queue is strict FIFO, so every fast scheduled job queued behind them. The casualty was not the photos but the LEADS: `PullP24LeadsJob`/`PullPpLeadsJob` run every 5 minutes, so each new portal check joined the back of a 5,300-job line — portal_leads 660 and 661 were enquired at 16:59 and 17:01 and only landed in CoreX at 17:46. Health was NO help: 21 workers RUNNING, 0 failed jobs, and the backlog alarm recommended restarting `corex-worker-live:*`, which would have killed the in-flight download and changed nothing — the lane was not stalled, it was serial and oversubscribed. Same shape as the TranscribeVoiceNoteJob incident of 2026-08-27, same answer: slow bulk work does not share a lane with latency-sensitive scheduled work. `DownloadListingThumbnail` now sets `onQueue('thumbnails')`; new 4-proc `thumbnails` lane with `--memory=512` because `handle()` GD-decodes the full-res image before the 300px downscale; mirrored to `docs/infra/supervisor/` so it survives a host rebuild; registered in `config/queue_alerting.php` as a BATCH lane so a deep queue does not false-alarm but a stalled head still does. Deliberately NOT reused `p24images` — that lane is narrow on purpose to stay under P24's per-IP limiter, so borrowing it would have moved the block onto the P24 import gallery pipeline. Measured on live: 0.65 -> 2.42 jobs/sec, `default` back to empty, pending lead checks ran in 868ms/915ms, 0 failures.)

> Prior: 2026-09-10 by **A rental wishlist showed ZERO properties while the agency held stock that plainly fitted — four independent filter defects in one engine, plus two of Johan's rulings** (contact 18900 / match 671, a 2-bed rental R4 000-R10 000 across nine South Coast suburbs, returned nothing. FOUR separate defects in `MatchingService`, each able to empty the page alone: (1) the per-listing-type status check was a WHITELIST that never contained `to_let` — CoreX's own canonical on-market rental status — so a whitelist failing CLOSED silently deleted 19 of the agency's 26 live rentals before scoring, and took `under_offer`/`on_show` and every agency-defined status with it; now a BLACKLIST of only the OTHER market's statuses, so an unknown status fails OPEN; (2) `NON_MATCHABLE_STATUSES` was a hand-maintained copy of `Property::OFF_MARKET_STATUSES` that had already drifted — missing `prospecting`/`not_selling`, so unmandated ingested stock was matchable; now DERIVED from the model constant, exactly as that constant's own docblock instructs; (3) the SQL query widened price by ±30% and then called `score()` with NO band, so the price hard gate re-cut at the exact stated ceiling and returned 0 — the relaxed near-miss surfacing in spec §5.1 had never once worked; (4) the SQL numeric pre-filter tolerated NULL but not 0, while `score()` has always read 0 as "incomplete listing data" — a captured-but-unpriced listing was deleted as though it cost nothing and fell below every buyer's minimum, and that is what hid the one property that genuinely fitted. All four mirrored into `matchSurvivesFilters()`, the hand-kept PHP twin the class docs require to stay in lockstep. **Johan's two rulings, asked as business questions, not engineering ones:** parent-area suburbs ON — P24 files "Margate Beach" and "Uvongo Beach" as suburbs entirely separate from "Margate" and "Uvongo", so a beachfront wishlist now also matches its parent suburb (child→parent ONLY, word-boundary prefix, scoped per `p24_city_id` because a bare name is not unique countrywide); and under-offer stock stays HIDDEN from buyers (matching-only, deliberately NOT added to `Property::OFF_MARKET_STATUSES`, so `under_offer` remains on-market for dashboards and the website). Fixing (3) then exposed that `priceFitRatio()` paid FULL marks anywhere inside the band — a live R11 400 rental scored 100 against a R10 000 ceiling, a worse version of the very complaint that created the price gate on 2026-08-11 — so it now decays from the stated bound to the band edge; callers passing no band are byte-for-byte unaffected. Match 671 now returns 4: Margate 2-bed R6 940 (100), Uvongo 3-bed R9 950 (100), Margate 3-bed R11 400 (81, correctly decayed for being over budget), St Michaels On Sea 2-bed (60). Swept all 608 active matches on live: 433 return results, 2 111 rows, ZERO off-market leaks, ZERO listing-type leaks, ZERO under-offer rows, ZERO rows outside the requested-plus-parent suburbs. > Prior: 2026-09-07 by **A document called "IDs / Identity" 500'd every time anyone opened it — a slash in a stored filename is not a display problem, it is an unopenable file** (Symfony's Content-Disposition builder rejects `/` and `\` outright, so `original_name` carrying one threw on EVERY download and inline view of that document — all 11+ serve call-sites at once, not just the FICA compliance-review pack it was reported against. The bytes were always fine. Source: the PDF splitter composes "Subject · DocType · Date.pdf" and the document-type label is literally `IDs / Identity`, so the slash landed inside the filename; 41 live documents affected from 19 Aug onward, and every new ID document the splitter filed was born broken. Fixed as a CLASS at the one gate every writer passes: `Document::sanitizeOriginalName()` + a `setOriginalNameAttribute` mutator, so no code path anywhere can reintroduce a bad name and not one serve call-site had to be touched — the same write-side shape as the 2026-08-30 listing-type fix, chosen for the same reason. The splitter also sanitises its baseName BEFORE its duplicate-name check, or that check would compare a form that is never stored and file two identically-named documents. Data migration repairs the written rows, soft-deleted included, and travels to Staging/QA1/demo. Live on `Prod` at Johan's explicit order; commit `bf2dbf4ac`; the exact failing request now 200s, all 2,915 live documents build a valid header, 0 names carry a separator, rows backed up to `storage/app` before the repair. No `schema:dump` owed — the migration is data-only and adds no DDL. `DocumentOriginalNameSanitizationTest` written but NOT run on the live host (no `TEST_DB_DATABASE` there); every assertion executed against the real model and passes. **PUSH TO GITHUB IS OUTSTANDING** — blocked by a tooling permission, so `origin/Prod` is one commit behind the live checkout.)

> Prior: 2026-08-31 by **Agents were being pushed "Listed 47 days ago, no documents on file" about the same listing ~4x a day, forever — the property scanner's dedup key was a clock bucket, not a fact** (`ScanPropertyNotifications` passed `now()->startOfHour()` as `threshold_hit_at`; the dispatcher only suppresses a repeat when an existing log row has `threshold_hit_at >= $thresholdHit`, so a fresh hourly key never matched and the ledger was inert. The 6h cooldown was the only brake left, capping it at ~4/day/pair instead of stopping it — 23,792 alerts, 26 May → 31 Aug, still firing when found; Maggie Venter was taking ~39 pushes a day about 19 listings. Same defect as the 1.9M `contact.fica_missing` storm, in the next call site along, while `NotificationDispatcherDedupTest` — which names the time-bucket trap explicitly — sat green, because it proved the DISPATCHER honours a stable key and nothing proved the SCANNER passed one. Key is now `$property->created_at->startOfDay()`: one alert per listing per agent, then silence. Self-healing — existing log rows already satisfy the stable key, so all 45 live pairs went quiet on deploy with no data edit. Live on `Prod` on Johan's explicit order; verified by running the real command on live: 0 new dispatches. `ScanPropertyDocumentsMissingDedupTest` written but NOT runnable on the live host (no test schema) — see Recent decisions log. **REPORTED, NOT TOUCHED:** `property_documents` does not exist and never has, so the "has documents?" check is permanently false — 40 of the 45 nagged listings DO have documents; and `ScanDealNotifications:107` carries the identical clock-bucket key.)

> Prior: 2026-08-27 by **AT-384 — the calendar opens on TODAY, not on last month** (agent report: opening the calendar in August landed on July. Root cause: the continuous month stream anchored on the Monday of the month's FIRST week — for any month whose 1st is not a Monday that week belongs to the previous month, and the sticky label names a week by its Thursday, so 27 Aug 2026 opened on the week of Mon 27 Jul labelled "July". Anchor precedence is now explicit `?date=` → today's week → the month's first week; the paged month grid is byte-identical. 9 tests / 44 assertions green in `CalendarContinuousScrollTest`; branch `AT-384`; see Recent decisions log and 3.2 IN FLIGHT).

> _Prior:_ 2026-07-14 by **MIXED-CASE COLUMNS ARE ADVERTISING THE WRONG THING — a rental "For Sale" (94 listings) AND a SOLD property "For Sale" (60 listings). LIVE `/corex` + Staging; hotfixes `dee61c28` + `de1c10bd`.** Two fixes, one root cause, found one after the other. **(1)** Property 6075 ("…to let in Uvongo") showed a **For Sale** badge on its live preview — the blade hardcoded `'active' => 'For Sale'` and never consulted `listing_type`, so **every** active rental was advertised for sale; the price card said "Asking price" over a monthly rent and a **bond calculator** costed a bond on R13,900. `Property::isRental()` is now THE predicate (case-insensitive, whole vocabulary — the P24 CSV importer writes a capitalised `'Rental'` that every case-sensitive check missed). **(2)** Verifying (1) on staging surfaced the worse twin: **`properties.status` is mixed-case too** ('Active' 444 rows, 'Sold' **60**, 'Rented' 1 — the P24 sync capitalises), and every badge compared it case-sensitively, so **60 SOLD properties were badged "For Sale" on the pickers and "FOR SALE" on generated ad cards**, a tenanted property advertised "TO LET", and 1,703 listings fell through the preview's badge map to a raw DB value shown to clients ("Let_out", "Under_offer"). `Property::normalizedStatus()` is now THE way to read status; `statusBadge()`, `adData()` and the preview badge all route through it; `rented`/`let_out` finally count as concluded (the rental "sold"). `pending` deliberately keeps its own badge (Johan's call). Verified on live across every stored casing. **RULE, now twice-proven: a CoreX enum column is not normalised on write — never compare one case-sensitively; go through the model's canonical reader.** **Tests not run — no dev deps on the server; `dev-check.ps1` owes `PropertyLivePreviewListingTypeTest` a run on Windows.**

Same-pillar bookkeeping (Property acting on Property) — confirmed this does NOT need the domain-events pattern, same reasoning/precedent as the existing `p24_stats_synced_at` column this migration is modelled on. New badge on the property card's top-right corner (top-left was the literal ask, but it's already occupied by the selection checkbox — confirmed the placement trade-off with Johan first), shown only when the count is `> 0`, with a "last generated" tooltip. Verified via Tinker through the real `generate()` endpoint (count 0→1, timestamp stamped, `index()` payload shape correct) AND a new dedicated test suite. New `size_or_land` resolves floor size if present, else `erf_size_m2` (newly exposed via `adData()`'s raw `floor_size_m2`/`land_size_m2`, alongside the existing pre-formatted `size_m2` left untouched for backward compat), else hidden — same shape as `garages_or_parking`. Land size gets a deliberate `" Erf"` suffix so it's never mistaken for floor size in the copy. **The bigger finding, while investigating why "it just shows 450 m² even on vacant land":** `textValue()`'s GENERIC fallback (every field that isn't beds/baths/garages/parking/Agent-2 — `reference`, `address`, `agent_phone`, `agency_name`, `website`, `size_m2` itself, any custom field) returned its design-time PREVIEW TEXT whenever the real property field was empty, **regardless of `opts.placeholders`** — meaning any of those fields, on any property missing that data, was rendering fabricated data (a fake reference number, a fake phone number, a fake address) on the ACTUAL GENERATED AD, indistinguishable from real. Fixed by generalising the guard that Agent-2 and the numeric feature fields already had (`if (!opts.placeholders) return '';`) to the one function that governs everything else — "fix the class, not the instance" (BUILD_STANDARD §6). > _Prior:_ 2026-08-02 by **Ad Manager generator — Agent Image "stretch" root-caused + fixed; dead platform-selector for custom templates fixed.** Follow-up to the same-day investigation below, run as `MODE:INVESTIGATION` first (qa1-only, zero code changes) per the Conductor & Lane Intake Protocol, THEN built once Johan confirmed the diagnosis. **Root cause: `html2canvas` 1.4.1 has documented gaps in CSS `object-fit` support** — it can rasterise an `<img>` at its raw intrinsic aspect ratio stretched to the box instead of correctly cropping/letterboxing it, and every image field in the kernel uses precisely the pattern this hits (`overflow:hidden` frame + `object-fit:cover` child). Traced every line touching the reported element's geometry in both the live preview and the capture and found NO code path that treats them differently — ruling out a data/logic bug in this app; the gap is in the rasteriser itself. **Fix (same principle as the brochure's GD-drawn pin — §10c: don't fight a rasteriser's known gap, pre-compute the correct pixels):** new kernel `objectFitRect()` (pure cover/contain crop geometry, unit-tested) + `prepareImagesForCapture()`, which pre-bakes the SAME crop onto an offscreen canvas for every `cover`/`contain` `<img>` and swaps its `src` to that pre-cropped PNG immediately before `html2canvas` runs — by capture time there's nothing left for object-fit handling to get wrong. Always restored via a returned restore function in `finally`, so the live preview/"change photo"/"reset to original" never see the swap. **Fixed the CLASS, not the instance (BUILD_STANDARD §6):** grepped every `html2canvas(` call site — there are THREE, not one (`ad.blade.php` `_capture()`, `ad-builder.blade.php` `capture()`, `tools/ad-manager.blade.php` `downloadRow()`) — all three now call it; the Ad Builder's own `capture()` was ALSO missing the `backgroundRemovalsSettled()` await the other two already had, fixed alongside it. **Separately, the dead platform-selector UI found during investigation:** the Facebook/Instagram/Story/WhatsApp buttons stayed clickable and "active"-looking for a custom template even though `cfg`'s custom-template branch never reads `platform` — now hidden entirely for `template==='custom'`, replaced with the template's real read-only size + a "Change in Ad Builder →" link (new `_customTemplateId`). Spec `.ai/specs/ad-manager.md` §16.1/§16.2. `tests/js/ad-render-kernel.mjs` 88/88 (8 new); `AdRenderKernelTest.php` 5/5. **LIVE on `main`/production (`php8.4-fpm` reloaded) AND merged to `Staging` (`php8.2-fpm` reloaded).**
> _Prior:_ 2026-08-02 by **Ad Manager generator — fixed the "zoom" glitch + a canvas-size bug, added custom-template picker previews; Agent Image "stretch" investigated, not conclusively closed.** Johan reported 3 issues generating from a real custom template ("For Sale Template") plus a 4th ask (picker previews); inspected the actual saved `layout_json` via Tinker rather than guessing. **Zoom glitch (confirmed + fixed):** `_capture()` briefly removes `#ad-scale-wrapper`'s CSS transform so html2canvas can grab native-size pixels, but the wrapper sits inside an `overflow:hidden` box sized to the small scaled-down preview — so the user saw the design snap to a cropped, zoomed-in corner for the whole capture window, then snap back. Fixed with a `capturingPreview` veil (`data-html2canvas-ignore`, spinner) wrapped in `try/finally` so a capture error can no longer leave the preview stuck un-scaled. **Canvas-size bug (confirmed + fixed, general case):** `get cfg()` used `platforms[canvasPreset]` (the preset's STANDARD size) instead of the template's own saved `canvasW`/`canvasH` whenever the preset name happened to match a real platform key — `canvasPreset` is just a stale label from whichever preset button was last clicked, not necessarily in sync after a manual resize. Fixed to always trust the template's own dimensions. (This specific template's `canvasPreset` is literally `"custom"`, not a real platform key, so ITS OWN cfg already resolved correctly by luck — this fix protects every other custom template that started from a real preset and was then resized.) **Agent Image "stretch" — investigated, not conclusively resolved:** the saved element is `shapeType:"rectangle"` (no masking at all), `objectFit:"cover"` (crops, doesn't distort), `removeBackground:true`; nothing in the render code was found to alter its aspect ratio. Two live possibilities, neither confirmed without a real browser: it was actually the zoom/clip glitch above (now fixed) happening to the whole canvas, or a background-removal cutout artifact (fixed twice already today) being described as "stretched" informally. Flagged, not patched blind. **Built — custom template picker previews:** cards showed only a letter avatar; now each mounts `CoreXAd.renderLayout()` live via a new `customThumbStyle()` "contain" fit (not the fixed-ratio pre-built cards use, since a custom template can be any size/ratio). Spec `.ai/specs/ad-manager.md` §16. No kernel changes — pure `ad.blade.php`; existing kernel tests still 80/80 unaffected. **LIVE on `main`/production (`php8.4-fpm` reloaded) AND merged to `Staging` (`php8.2-fpm` reloaded).**
> _Prior:_ 2026-08-02 by **Ad Builder — "Remove background" round 2: the first fix's safety floor overcorrected, removed.** Same day, same photo, second report: after fixing "it ate her shirt" (round 1 — bad seeding + bad colour sampling), Johan reported the fix now "left like 20% at the bottom" un-removed. **Root cause: round 1's hard floor** (`noRemoveBelow = h*0.82`, "never erase the bottom ~18% no matter what") **was blind** — it had no way to tell backdrop from clothing in that band, and Retha's actual photo has genuine visible backdrop below the shoulders (not every crop has the garment touching the literal bottom pixel). **The floor is removed.** Propagation is unrestricted by y-position once seeded, so genuine connected backdrop is correctly cleared all the way down again — what actually stops a real garment from being eaten is not seeding the ambiguous bottom edge, sampling backdrop colour from a clean (top-only) spot, and a tolerance tight enough to catch a real colour difference — all kept from round 1, just without the blunt height-based patch on top. **Honest remaining limit, stated not hidden:** a garment truly colour-IDENTICAL to the backdrop (zero edge) still has no signal to key off and will still get swept in — no colour algorithm can do better without real segmentation, and a blind floor papering over that ONE synthetic worst case is exactly what broke the much more common real case. Three-shape test coverage added: real-colour-difference garment survives at the bottom edge; genuine low-frame backdrop is removed; colour-identical garment is knowingly swept in (asserted, not a surprise). Spec `.ai/specs/ad-manager.md` §15.1. `tests/js/ad-render-kernel.mjs` 80/80; `AdRenderKernelTest.php` 5/5. **LIVE on `main`/production (`php8.4-fpm` reloaded) AND merged to `Staging` (`php8.2-fpm` reloaded).**
> _Prior:_ 2026-08-02 by **Ad Builder — "Remove background" ate a real agent's white shirt, fixed.** Johan reported it live: removing the background of Retha's photo also stripped the white off her shirt. **Root cause: the flood fill seeded from the ENTIRE frame border, including the bottom row** — a headshot crop routinely has the shoulders/shirt reaching the bottom edge, and a white shirt on a white/light backdrop is the COMMON case, not an edge case, so the fill flowed straight from backdrop into garment with no colour edge to stop at. **Three fixes:** (1) seeds no longer include the bottom row, side-column seeding restricted to the upper half; (2) a hard floor — the bottom ~18% of the frame (`noRemoveBelow = h*0.82`) is NEVER erased regardless of connectivity, because restricting seeds alone doesn't stop the fill reaching a garment via legitimate propagation from higher up — only an explicit height floor does; (3) the backdrop-colour sample now uses the TOP-LEFT/TOP-RIGHT corners only (bottom corners are often clothing, and averaging them in had been pulling the "backdrop colour" toward the shirt's colour), tolerance tightened 40→26 now the sample is cleaner. **Trade-off stated, not silent:** genuine backdrop sitting low in a loose/half-body crop is now ALSO left un-removed — failing to clear background is the safe failure direction; eating the subject is not. New named regression test reproduces the EXACT reported shape (light garment touching the bottom edge) plus asserts the stated trade-off explicitly. Spec `.ai/specs/ad-manager.md` §15.1. `tests/js/ad-render-kernel.mjs` 80/80 (4 new); `AdRenderKernelTest.php` 5/5. **LIVE on `main`/production (`php8.4-fpm` reloaded) AND merged to `Staging` (`php8.2-fpm` reloaded).**

The Design tab now shows a "Grouped — N elements" block whenever the selection `selIsGroup`: a list of every member (new `selGroupMembers` getter) each with a small ✕ — `ungroupOne(el)` clears `groupId` on JUST that one, leaving the rest of the group intact — plus an "Ungroup All" button (the existing whole-group action). Removing down to a single leftover member dissolves its `groupId` too, since a "group" of one is meaningless state. Pure Blade/Alpine, no kernel changes, no effect on rendered ads. > _Prior:_ 2026-08-02 by **Ad Builder — Agent Image "Remove background."** A new checkbox on the Agent Image element cuts a plain/solid-colour backdrop (the request's own example: a white studio background) so only the person remains. **Not AI/ML segmentation** — no model, no third-party API, nothing leaves the browser — it's a flood-fill: sample the backdrop colour from the four corners, then flood-fill transparency inward from every BORDER pixel that colour-matches within a tolerance, stopping at sharp colour edges. A white shirt collar in the MIDDLE of the photo (not touching the border) survives; an actual solid backdrop is removed — proven by a synthetic-pixel test, no real Canvas/Image needed. **One `onload="window.CoreXAd.stripBackground(this)"` attribute is the only per-surface change** — fires identically whether the `<img>` was inserted via Alpine's `x-html` (Builder) or `renderLayout()`'s `innerHTML` (generator/bulk manager). Downscaled to 500px before processing (a full-res profile photo is never slow); cached per source URL (`_bgRemovalCache`) so the SAME agent's photo across a bulk run of many properties is only ever processed once; same-origin-only (degrades to showing the original photo, never breaks, if the canvas is CORS-tainted). Both capture paths (`ad.blade.php` `_capture()`, `tools/ad-manager.blade.php` `downloadRow()`) now `await CoreXAd.backgroundRemovalsSettled()` before `html2canvas` — a best-effort guarantee, not formally provable, flagged as such rather than assumed solved. Toggling the checkbox off reverts cleanly to the original photo (nothing on `Property`/`User` is ever mutated). Explicitly NOT built: adjustable tolerance, manual colour picking, edge feathering, and the "backgroundless" placeholder-box option from the same request (dropped mid-conversation on request). Spec `.ai/specs/ad-manager.md` §15.1. `tests/js/ad-render-kernel.mjs` 76/76; `AdRenderKernelTest.php` 5/5. **LIVE on `main`/production (`php8.4-fpm` reloaded) AND merged to `Staging` (`php8.2-fpm` reloaded).**

> _Prior:_ 2026-08-02 by **Ad Builder — element grouping (select/move/duplicate together).** Elements sharing a new `el.groupId` behave as one unit: a plain click on any member selects the whole group (`groupMembers()`/`expandToGroups()`, routed through by `elMouseDown()`, marquee mouseup, and `selectFromLayers()` alike), and the EXISTING multi-select drag/nudge/rotate/lock/delete logic then applies unchanged — grouping only changes how a selection gets FORMED, not what happens once it's formed. **Group** (`Ctrl G` / toolbar button) bundles 2+ selected into one new group (overwrites any prior group — no nesting); **Ungroup** (`Ctrl Shift G`) always clears the WHOLE group, never a partial slice, because selecting one member already expanded the selection to all of them. `selIsGroup` gates the toolbar's single toggling Group/Ungroup button (mirrors the Lock/Unlock pattern) and requires the selection to be the group's ENTIRE membership, not a shift-clicked subset — the tooltip never promises an Ungroup that would leave part of the group behind. **Duplicate/copy-paste remap group IDs** (`_remapGroups()`) so a cloned group becomes its own new group rather than silently re-merging with the original. `groupId` is **builder-only** (same treatment as `locked`, §12.3) — the kernel's `makeElement()` seeds it `null` for schema symmetry across all three ad surfaces, but the generator/bulk Ad Manager never read it, so a grouped element renders byte-identical to an ungrouped one; a legacy template (no `groupId` key) opens fully ungrouped. Spec `.ai/specs/ad-manager.md` §13.1. **Verified: JS syntax-checked (extracted + `node --check`) and Blade-compiled clean; NOT click-tested in an actual browser** (no browser available from this shell session) — recommend testing the group/ungroup/move/duplicate flow live before relying on it for a real template. **LIVE on `main`/production (`php8.4-fpm` reloaded) AND merged to `Staging` (`php8.2-fpm` reloaded).**
> _Prior:_ 2026-08-02 by **Ad Manager — Beds/Baths/Garages/Parking get "Number + Label" display + a real-estate icon picker + a combined "Garages / Parking" field for bulk runs.** The builder's numeric feature fields only ever showed a bare number. New per-element `numberFormat` ('number'|'label') renders e.g. "1 Bedroom"/"3 Bedrooms" with correct singular/plural (Parking never pluralises, matching the printable brochure's existing convention); new `icon`/`iconSize` render one of 12 curated real-estate SVGs (Bed/Bath/Garage/Parking reuse the brochure's proven paths) inline before the value, `fill="currentColor"` so it always follows the element's text colour. Also adds **Parking** as a 4th builder field (previously brochure-only) — `Property::spaceCount()` is now the single derivation both the brochure and `adData()` read, replacing a duplicated private method. New **`garages_or_parking`** field (§14.1) resolves per-property at render time — garages if the listing has any, else parking, else hidden — so ONE bulk-ad template reads correctly across a batch of mixed listings instead of printing blank/"0" on whichever source that listing lacks; needed zero backend/Blade changes since it just reuses the existing numeric-feature panel + `prop.garages`/`prop.parking`. **Fixed same day: the icon grid forced a horizontal scroll in the design panel** — `grid-template-columns:repeat(6,1fr)` doesn't let tracks shrink below an `<svg>`'s fallback intrinsic size; `minmax(0,1fr)` + `min-width:0` + `overflow:hidden` fixed it without resizing the panel. All three ad surfaces (Builder/generator/bulk Ad Manager) pick every bit of this up automatically — one shared kernel (§12). Default is OFF/bare-number for every element, including brand-new ones, so no saved template changes appearance. Spec `.ai/specs/ad-manager.md` §14/§14.1. `tests/js/ad-render-kernel.mjs` 57/57. **LIVE on `main`/production (`php8.4-fpm` reloaded) AND merged to `Staging` (`php8.2-fpm` reloaded)** — commits `937009e4`, `575ab9e8` on main; `8450fdc7`, `5c6c5ab4` on Staging. **Housekeeping flagged, not done here: this file is 378+ lines, already over the 350-line cap before this entry — needs a trim pass.**

Now seeded on agency creation + backfilled — **per group, only when that group is empty**, so a curated vocabulary is never reinstated.

> Previously: 2026-07-27 by **ELLIE EXTERNAL REFERENCE SOURCES — BUILT + LIVE, directly on `/corex` @ `main` (production), Johan's explicit call.** New narrow, structural exception to "Ellie has no internet access" (spec `.ai/specs/ellie-reference-sources.md`, approved same day): a super_admin-only admin page (`/admin/ellie/reference-sources`, permission `manage_reference_sources` — deliberately excluded from admin's all-minus-exclude default, same treatment as `agency_setup.track`) lets an admin paste ONE approved URL; CoreX fetches, extracts, chunks and embeds it (reusing the existing self-hosted BGE pipeline, `ellie_reference_sources`/`ellie_reference_chunks` tables) and a new `search_reference_sites` tool lets Ellie search ONLY that pre-indexed content — tried after `search_knowledge`/pillar tools, cites the source URL, never falls back to general model knowledge. **The safety property that actually matters: no tool given to the model can trigger a fetch.** Fetching only ever happens from an admin action or the new daily `ellie:refresh-reference-sources` cron (05:30) — there is no chat-reachable code path to the network at all, which closes prompt-injection-driven SSRF structurally rather than by allowlist-checking alone. SSRF guards (`EllieReferenceSourceFetchService`): scheme + DNS/IP-range check (PHP's `FILTER_FLAG_NO_PRIV_RANGE|FILTER_FLAG_NO_RES_RANGE`) re-run on EVERY fetch (blocks DNS rebinding, not just at add-time), every redirect hop re-validated before being followed (`curl` auto-redirects disabled), content-type + 5MB size cap, `CURLOPT_RESOLVE` pins the connection to the already-validated IP. **A real bug the tests caught, not a hypothetical:** `validateAddable()` — the add-time guard the controller calls BEFORE creating a source row — only checked URL shape (scheme/host), never actually called the IP-resolution guard; every literal private/loopback/link-local IP (127.0.0.1, 10.x, 169.254.169.254, `::1`, `localhost`) silently passed. 19 new tests (`EllieReferenceSourceFetchServiceTest` + `SearchToolTest`) caught it on first run — 6 failing — fixed by having `validateAddable()` call `resolveSafePublicIp()` too, not just `guardUrl()`; re-ran, 19/19 green (37 assertions). **Infra notes for whoever's next on this box:** this session discovered mid-build that it was operating directly on the PRODUCTION server (`91.99.130.85`, `APP_ENV=production`) rather than a QA clone — flagged to Johan, who chose to continue here rather than move to QA1/QA2. Production runs `composer install --no-dev` (no phpunit/mockery) AND had no `hfc_dash_test` MySQL schema/grant at all (test suite had literally never run on this box) — both fixed to run the new tests (dev deps installed, tests run, `composer install --no-dev` restored after; `hfc_dash_test` DB + grant created via `sudo mysql`, left in place for future test runs on this box, never touches `nexus_os`). Migrations are pure-additive (2 new tables, nothing altered), `schema:dump` refreshed. **The rule: an add-time validator that only checks shape and not substance is worse than none — it tells the caller "you're safe" while the real guard is somewhere else entirely, waiting to be reached (or not).**

Team = headcount × R450 (≤10); Agency = R1 495 base + **graduated** tiers (295/250/195) — **25 users = R7 920**, asserted verbatim, because a flat 25×R195 is R6 370 and nobody would catch a R1 550/mo error until an agency queried an invoice. Extra branches R750, first free, **both plans** (the plans are price shapes, not feature sets — Johan). No VAT. **A seat is ANY active user** (a login is a seat); **the count is read LIVE and never stored**, so the bill cannot go stale no matter what changed a user — events + the nightly sweep exist only to make the plan-change **email** prompt, never to keep the **number** right. The flip is a **compare-and-set** (one email per switch, never two); the discount **expires by arithmetic** (no cron can forget it); custom-vs-discount is guarded three deep. Owner page is `owner_only` with **no permission key on purpose** — a key is grantable, and one mis-click shows an agency admin every competitor's rate. **Phase 2 (the actual cutoff/lockout) deliberately NOT built** — specced to reuse `maintenance_mode`; you cannot gate on money the system can't yet name. **Needs Johan: spec sign-off + confirm nothing belongs in the onboarding wizard (§14).** _Prior:_ **MIXED-CASE COLUMNS ARE ADVERTISING THE WRONG THING — a rental "For Sale" (94 listings) AND a SOLD property "For Sale" (60 listings). LIVE `/corex` + Staging; hotfixes `dee61c28` + `de1c10bd`.** Two fixes, one root cause, found one after the other. **(1)** Property 6075 ("…to let in Uvongo") showed a **For Sale** badge on its live preview — the blade hardcoded `'active' => 'For Sale'` and never consulted `listing_type`, so **every** active rental was advertised for sale; the price card said "Asking price" over a monthly rent and a **bond calculator** costed a bond on R13,900. `Property::isRental()` is now THE predicate (case-insensitive, whole vocabulary — the P24 CSV importer writes a capitalised `'Rental'` that every case-sensitive check missed). **(2)** Verifying (1) on staging surfaced the worse twin: **`properties.status` is mixed-case too** ('Active' 444 rows, 'Sold' **60**, 'Rented' 1 — the P24 sync capitalises), and every badge compared it case-sensitively, so **60 SOLD properties were badged "For Sale" on the pickers and "FOR SALE" on generated ad cards**, a tenanted property advertised "TO LET", and 1,703 listings fell through the preview's badge map to a raw DB value shown to clients ("Let_out", "Under_offer"). `Property::normalizedStatus()` is now THE way to read status; `statusBadge()`, `adData()` and the preview badge all route through it; `rented`/`let_out` finally count as concluded (the rental "sold"). `pending` deliberately keeps its own badge (Johan's call). Verified on live across every stored casing. **RULE, now twice-proven: a CoreX enum column is not normalised on write — never compare one case-sensitively; go through the model's canonical reader.** **Tests not run — no dev deps on the server; `dev-check.ps1` owes `PropertyLivePreviewListingTypeTest` a run on Windows.**

The preview blade mapped status→badge with a hardcoded `'active' => 'For Sale'` and **never consulted `listing_type` at all**, so every active rental was advertised for sale on the page an agent sends to a client. Two more sale-assumptions sat on the same page: the price card read **"Asking price"** over a monthly rent, and a **bond calculator** offered the tenant a repayment schedule on a purchase that will never happen (on 6075 it costed a bond on R13,900). **The class under the instance:** "is this a rental?" was answered inline in **six places with divergent vocabularies and case-sensitive comparisons** — `statusBadge()`/`adData()` took `rental|to_let`, `effectivePrice()` lowercased but took only `rental`, the P24/PP deep-link builders took only lowercase `rental`, two blades rolled their own, the website API took `rental|to_let|to-let|lease` — **while `P24ListingsCsvParser` writes a capitalised `'Rental'`**, which every one of those checks would have missed. **`Property::isRental()` is now THE predicate** (case-insensitive, whole vocabulary, one place); every check routes through it. The case-tolerance is **preventive** — no row carries the capitalised spelling today, but the importer that writes it is live. Verified by rendering the real view for 6075: badge **To Let**, **Monthly rental R 13 900 / month**, no calculator; the sale control still reads For Sale / Asking price and keeps its calculator; confirmed on the live URL after php8.4-fpm reload. **The preview page had no test at all — which is how it shipped**; `PropertyLivePreviewListingTypeTest` now pins the badge, the price label, the calculator's absence, dead-status precedence, and the full vocabulary incl. the importer casing. **Tests not run on the server — no dev deps there; `dev-check.ps1` owes them a run on Windows.**

Staging⊇main holds by content (2 SHA-drift syndication hotfixes on main already in Staging via `89474597`+`PpLandCategoryTest`). **Three items, one model:** (1) **Explicit-save default retires the debounced auto-persist** — `window.CoreXCal` three tiers: SAVED DEFAULT (DB, every fresh load, written ONLY by "Save as my default" = `saveCockpit` repurposed to one atomic full-arrangement write) · TRANSIENT (sessionStorage, every in-session change synchronous, survives navigate-away-and-return, discarded on hard reload via `performance.navigation.type`) · FACTORY/ROLE fallback. Auto-persist writers removed (default_view on ?view=, persistCockpit debounce, panel/deck/layer toggles → transient only; deck-add via new non-persisting `GET /calendar/tile/{id}`). Existing per-user rows = initial saved default (no migration). (2) **POPPING-PANELS BUG root-caused + DEAD** — cause: every change debounced (400–450ms) to the DB default; hide-panel + navigate-within-window abandoned the write → panel returned. Now synchronous transient → survives navigation; reload → saved default. (3) **Calendar scrolling continuous|paged** (per-user `scroll_mode` in cockpit JSON, no migration; paged = classic single-month/single-week prev-next reusing the SAME `_week-row`/`_day-column` — one rendering truth, two nav shells) **+ alternating month-boundary tint** (continuous only, owning-month=Thursday, ON TOP OF the 2px seam + "Jul 1" + sticky header; one tunable CSS var `--cal-month-tint-alpha`=0.045). Cockpit hardening + frozen `MobileCalendarController` envelope untouched. **Johan QA re-checks (both shipped): (a)** Save/Reset moved from the deck header to the ALWAYS-VISIBLE top toolbar (they used to vanish with the deck → couldn't save a deck-hidden default). **(b) SAVE FEEDBACK:** Save flips to "Saved ✓" + canonical CoreX toast `window.showToast('Calendar default saved')` (my earlier `corex-toast` event had NO listener = silent nothing); failure path fixed — `save()` now checks `r.ok` (fetch does NOT reject on 4xx/5xx → the old `.then()` reported 500 as success) → "Save failed" + error toast, transient kept, no false success; Reset → cross-reload flash toast. > _Prior:_ 2026-07-05 by **PP-B3 RESOLVED — 5 attributes were emitted in the WRONG PP Appendix A datatype → LIVE `main` + Staging**. The audit's highest-priority open item (potential PP106 whole-listing rejection) was settled with a **read-only `GetFullDetailsOfAllListingsByBranch` call against the live HFC branch (327 listings)** — the authoritative datatype ground truth. Result: `Kitchen`(191/191 stored `"Yes"`), `Family_TV_Room`(11), `Study`(13), `StaffQuarters`(16), `Entrance_hall`(37) are all **boolean `"Yes"`** in Appendix A, but `PrivatePropertyListingMapper::buildAttributes()` emitted them as **integer counts** in the `$counts` loop — a datatype mismatch. **Why it never visibly broke:** PP *coerces* a numeric value to `"Yes"` for boolean attributes (the ONLY reason property 6049 survived with `Kitchen="1"` — the audit's "Kitchen is a proven count" anchor was WRONG; PP silently coerced it). The 2026-07-02 PP106s were the *reverse* mismatch (old `EnSuite="true"` boolean → integer attr, since fixed to `EnSuite="1"`). **Fix:** the five moved out of `$counts` into present-only `"Yes"` flags (keyed off `Kitchen`/`TV Room`/`Study`+`Office`/`Domestic Room`/`Entrance Hall` spaces); `Lounges`/`DiningAreas`/`Parking`/`Carports` stay integer counts (read-back-confirmed 1–3). Tinker-verified: buildAttributes now emits Kitchen/Family_TV_Room/Study/StaffQuarters/Entrance_hall=`"Yes"`, Lounges/DiningAreas/Parking/Carports=int. `GetFullDetailsOfAllListingsByBranch` is the standard read-back to settle any future count-vs-flag question (WSDL op exists; called via the client's generic `call()`). Files: `PrivatePropertyListingMapper.php`, audit doc (PP-B3 → RESOLVED). No migration/route/perm. ⚠️ live box is `--no-dev` → run `PpFeatureAttributesTest` on laragon.

> _Prior:_ 2026-07-05 by **SYNDICATION MAPPING FIXES (5) → LIVE `main` + Staging**. From the 2026-07-05 syndication-mapping audit (`.ai/audits/syndication-mapping-audit-2026-07-05.md`), the five confirmed "fix-now" items landed. **PP (`PrivatePropertyListingMapper`):** (PP-B1) `Carports` was dead code — `countSpaces($property,'Carport')` but a carport is a FEATURE of the `Parking` space, not a space type, so it always returned 0 and never syndicated → new `countCarports()` sums Parking bays/units carrying the `Carport` feature (space-level featuresAll ⇒ bay count, else per-unit hits; tinker-proven 2/2/0). (PP-G1) seven amenity flags (Gym/Clubhouse/TennisCourt/SquashCourt/Jaccuzzi/Jetty_Berth/Storage) only checked `has()` feature strings, so entered as a SPACE they never fired → OR-ed the matching `hasSpace(<type>)` (Gym/Clubhouse/Tennis Court/Squash Court/Jacuzzi/Jetty/Storeroom, all verified against `config/property-spaces.php`). **P24 (`Property24ListingMapper`):** (P24-B1) `showLocation` used `(bool)($lat && $lng)` but lat/lng are `decimal:7` string casts ("0.0000000" is truthy) → never-geocoded listings emitted `showLocation:true` (privacy-adjacent) → numeric guard mirroring the proven line-82 `geographicLocation` fix. > _Prior:_ 2026-07-04 by **P24 SUBMIT-TIMEOUT HOTFIX — a transient Property24 timeout no longer clobbers a LIVE listing to 'error' → LIVE `main`**. Symptom (Johan): property **1357** (ref **117201765**) showed `P24 Ref: 117201765 — Error  Error: Connection failed: cURL error 28: Operation timed out after 120000ms with 0 bytes received` — a listing that was STILL LIVE on the portal, mislabelled broken; **5 submit timeouts on 2026-07-04** (P24-side slowness — 0 bytes back within the 120s read timeout; today also 16 connect-timeouts on harmless `status_check`). ROOT CAUSE: `Property24SyndicationService::performSubmit()` wrote a permanent `p24_syndication_status='error'` + the raw cURL string into `p24_last_error` on ANY save failure — INCLUDING a transient timeout (no HTTP status). The `isTransientFailure()` classifier already existed and was correctly used by `deactivate/reactivate` (AT-P24 #5) but **never by the submit/update path**. An `error` row additionally (a) lost every UI recovery button (View/Refresh/Deactivate require active|submitted|submitting) and (b) was excluded from `syncAllActivations()` reconcile → **stranded**. FIX (root-cause, mirrors AT-P24 #5): `performSubmit` now branches transient→`handleTransientSubmitFailure()` — **has p24_ref → keep 'active'** (it's still live) + amber "Sync deferred — still live; tap Refresh to retry" note; **no ref → 'pending'** (retryable), never a hard error. Permanent 4xx (validation) → unchanged crimson `error`. UI (`properties/show.blade.php`): sync-state poller no longer shows a FALSE "success" toast when a deferred note is present, and a new amber status line surfaces the note on reload (buttons stay). REMEDIATION: property 1357 verified live via a real `isOnPortal` call (`data:true`) → reset to `active`/enabled, error cleared. TEST: `tests/Feature/Syndication/Property24SubmitTransientFailureTest.php` (2 cases: live-listing timeout stays active / first-submit timeout → pending). ⚠️ the LIVE `/corex` box is `composer install --no-dev` (no phpunit/collision) → this test must be RUN on dev/laragon via `dev-check.ps1`; here it was `php -l`-clean and the transient classification tinker-verified (timeout/503→transient, 422→permanent). Files: `Property24SyndicationService.php`, `resources/views/corex/properties/show.blade.php`, new test. No migration, no new route/perm.

Prevent-or-absorb: a rule whose party the deal lacks, or whose document can't resolve, is SKIPPED with a visible note. **`SecureDocumentController`** — PUBLIC tokened flow: open link (logged) → OTP to the recipient's OWN email (canonical `App\Services\Otp\OtpService`, purpose `deal_doc_distribution`, audit→access_log) → session-verified → authenticated `Storage::download` (never a public path); revoked/expired shows a friendly 410 page. Branded `DealSecureLinkMail`/`DealDocumentDeliveryMail` extend `BaseSignatureMail`. New perms `deals_v2.distribute_documents` + `deals_v2.manage_distribution_rules`. UI: a "Distribute documents" modal + Sent list + revoke on the deal; a distribution-rules matrix editor settings page + sidebar nav + a pointer on the doc-types settings page. Test `DealDistributionTest` 6/6 (41 assertions); WS0 `DealPipelineEngineTest` 7/7 (no regression). Visibility chokepoint unchanged (`access_communication_archive`). Safe to flip — both paths were field-dormant (0 inbound/0 pending/0 devices/0 mailboxes), zero data-migration pressure. Tests: Email+WA ingestor rewritten for discard+owner (16 pass), reconciliation green (9). > _Prior:_ 2026-06-26 by **AT-106 — daily self-cleaning P24 location sync (stamp-and-sweep) → LIVE (direct to main)**. Answers "how do we keep provinces/cities/suburbs always correct so a phantom can't recur": `p24:sync-locations` now **stamps `p24_verified_at=now()` on every province/city/suburb P24 returns**, then **soft-deletes anything it did NOT return this run** (verified_at < runStart) — and is **scheduled daily at 11:00** (`routes/console.php`, `withoutOverlapping`; the old monthly entry removed as redundant). This makes the tree self-healing: removed P24 locations (the city-tier staleness AT-105 found — Durban North/785 etc.) get swept automatically; new ones appear. **Safety:** the full walk aborts on ANY fetch error before pruning, AND an **absolute sanity floor** (P24 must return ≥ 5 provinces / 50 cities / 3000 suburbs) skips the sweep on a truncated/auth-broken reply so an API blip can never wipe the tree (deliberately absolute, NOT a ratio-of-current-table — the table is bloated with stale rows the first sweep must clear). Pruned suburbs remediate dependent properties (FK chain nulled + `p24_suburb_mismatch=1`); pruned cities/provinces null the matching property FK. Soft-delete only (#1) → recoverable, and a returned location is revived via `withTrashed()->updateOrCreate`. **Per-agency runs do NOT prune** (`--no-prune` added to `AgencyController` cred-validation syncs + `SyncP24LocationsJob` when agency-scoped) — only the authoritative global daily run sweeps. New `--no-prune` flag on the command. Migration `2026_06_26_140000` adds `p24_verified_at` + soft-deletes to `p24_provinces`/`p24_cities` (suburbs already had them from AT-105) + backfills existing rows. Test `P24LocationSweepTest` (healthy run sweeps only unstamped rows / floor skips on a partial run). **FIRST SWEEP DONE on live** (manual run, observed): swept **197 stale cities + 6,108 suburbs** (6,107 stale + the AT-105 phantom); now 9 provinces / 616 cities / 20,754 suburbs active, **0 active rows left unstamped**, **267/267 syndicating listings kept their `p24_suburb_id`**, **0 properties remediated** (nothing depended on a pruned location). All soft-deleted → recoverable. **Resilience fix (commit `0df9cf9a`):** the first attempt aborted on a single cURL-28 timeout (P24 slow on one cityId) — by design it aborted BEFORE pruning (0 pruned, syndication intact), proving the safety. Every P24 fetch is now wrapped in `fetchRetry` (bounded exponential backoff on transient connection/5xx/429, fail-fast on definitive 4xx, still throws-before-prune if P24 is truly down). Supersedes the AT-105 "AT-106 follow-up" note (reconcile is now obsoleted by the sweep for routine upkeep; keep `p24:reconcile-suburbs` as a manual one-off). The importer page (`/admin/importer/p24-locations`) progress panel now shows a sweep summary on completion ("Swept N stale locations (X cities, Y suburbs) · Z properties remediated", or "Sweep skipped — tree left intact"). Commits: `ad190521` (sweep) + `cd6890c0` (schema) + `0df9cf9a` (retry) + UI line.

> _Prior:_ 2026-06-26 by **AT-105 — P24 phantom-suburb existence guard + reconcile → LIVE (direct to main)**. Closes the class behind AT-104's Addington/5997 phantom: `p24_suburbs` rows could carry a `p24_id` P24 never returns, and `AppliesP24Location` only checked the suburb→city→province chain was *internally consistent* — never that the `p24_id` actually exists on P24 (so the phantom passed, saved on prop 5747, P24 bounced it HTTP 400 "SuburbId is invalid"). Fix = one authoritative signal `p24_suburbs.p24_verified_at` ("P24 returned this exact p24_id under this city in the last sync/reconcile"). **(A)** migration adds the column + backfills every existing non-deleted row (= updated_at) so live saves keep working; `SyncP24Locations` now stamps `now()` on every updateOrCreate. **(B) guard:** `AppliesP24Location` rejects any suburb with NULL `p24_verified_at`; `P24LocationController::suburbs()` only offers verified rows (phantoms unselectable + unsaveable on BOTH property paths). **(C) cleanup:** a deterministic migration soft-deletes the proven phantom (matched by p24_id 5997 + name, not local row id) + remediates prop 5747 (FK chain nulled, `p24_suburb_mismatch=1` → agent re-picks; Addington is in P24 *nowhere*); new `php artisan p24:reconcile-suburbs {--apply}` live-diffs every city's P24 suburb list, stamps survivors, soft-deletes stale rows (cities whose fetch FAILS are skipped, never pruned on a network error) + remediates their properties — **dry-run by default**. Note: admin manual-add rows carry `p24_city_id=NULL` (already guard-rejected) and are never stamped verified, so that ingress stays closed without a fragile per-save live call. Test `tests/Feature/Syndication/P24SuburbVerifiedGuardTest.php` (3 paths: verified accepted / phantom rejected / endpoint hides phantom). **DEPLOYED on live** (commits `54afdb48` code + `67ccb1e9` schema snapshot, pushed to main): `migrate` ran A+B on prod (26,819 active rows backfilled verified; phantom 5997 soft-deleted + prop 5747 remediated to NULL/mismatch=1; 0 properties left orphaned); `schema:dump` committed; config/route/view/cache cleared; `queue:restart` + php8.{2,3,4}-fpm reloaded (live `/corex` worker respawned on fresh code). Guard verified on live (verified suburb accepted, unverified rejected). phpunit is a dev-dep absent on the box — run `P24SuburbVerifiedGuardTest` via the Windows dev-check. **Reconcile dry-run finding (NOT applied):** the suburb universe under live P24 cities is CLEAN (0 prunes across 86 sampled cities; the one phantom was already migration-purged), so `--apply` would change ~nothing now. BUT it exposed a **city-tier staleness**: ~most local `p24_cities` rows return P24 `400 "City with Id X does not exist"` (e.g. Durban North/785, Ezakheni/170) — stale ids P24 removed that `updateOrCreate` never prunes. The reconcile safely SKIPS them (never prunes a suburb on a city-fetch failure). **Recommended follow-up (AT-106):** extend reconcile to the city tier (soft-delete cities P24 no longer returns via getCities) and/or re-run `p24:sync-locations`, THEN a full `p24:reconcile-suburbs --apply`. New migrations: `2026_06_26_120000` (column) + `2026_06_26_120001` (phantom purge).

> _Prior:_ 2026-06-26 by **AT-104 — P24 suburb resolution hotfix → LIVE (direct to main, no staging)**. Collision suburb names were syndicating to the wrong P24 city — South Coast "Glenmore" (Port Edward, p24_id 10790) listings showed as "Durban" (10787). Root cause in `Property24ListingMapper::resolveSuburbId()`: tier 1 checked `pp_suburb_id` (a Private Property column NULL on every CoreX property → branch never fired), then fell through to `P24Suburb::lookup()->first()` — a bare name match, no city filter — picking whichever same-named row sorted first. Fix: trust the stored chain-verified `p24_suburb_id` FK first (fixes 100% of the 265 enabled listings); else name match **disambiguated by city/province**, returned only on a UNIQUE match; else `null` → `validate()` skips + flags ("Suburb unmapped — manual P24 suburb mapping required"); never a guessed fallback. Removed the name-only `/suburbs/find` auto-resolve + bare fuzzy fallback (could re-introduce the bug; zero enabled props relied on them; `p24:sync-locations` keeps `p24_suburbs` complete). Province text now normalises both sides (P24 stores "KwaZulu Natal", CoreX holds "KwaZulu-Natal"/"KZN"). Commit `0709f69d` on main; queue:restart + `corex-worker-live` restarted + booted clean (P24 sync 222/0 errors post-restart). **Verified on LIVE:** re-synced sample props 5747/5762/5831 → `request_payload.suburbId` now **10790** (was 10787), HTTP 200. New `tests/Feature/Syndication/Property24SuburbResolutionTest.php` (6 paths) committed — phpunit is a dev-dep absent on the live box, so verification was via tinker against the live DB. **⚠️ BACKFILL AWAITING JOHAN'S GO (do NOT auto-run):** 8 live listings still hold the old wrong suburbId — props **2020, 3894, 5860, 5862, 5891** (Glenmore 10787→10790), **2821** (Bulwer, Durban 14226→5969), **3307** (Rocklands, Port Edward 18357→18249), **5900** (Melville, Port Shepstone 4145→10881). 0 listings now resolve to null (nothing previously-syndicating gets skipped).
